## Utility resource plans (checklist row 37)

- Southwestern Public Service Company: 2023 IRP. Tracked plants matched: 4

## Checklist group 1. Validation report

Edits and questions that fix an error from the GEM validation report.

### G100001090367 / Lea County Data Center power station / Phase 1
*question about existing data; checklist rows 7 (Validation report errors fixed)*
- about: validation
- question: Is there a document that states Backup Power for the Lea County Data Center?
- finding: The company reports no backup engine design, and no permit exists in the New Mexico Environment Department harvest.
- recommendation: I searched the company release https://www.businesswire.com/news/home/20251106765936/en/New-Era-Energy-Digital-Enters-into-Land-Option-Purchase-Agreement-for-3500-Acres-in-New-Mexico-for-7GW-AI-Data-Center-Hub-Marks-first-Wholly-Owned-Development-Separate-from-TCDC-Joint-Venture, the news article https://www.abqjournal.com/business/texas-company-eyes-lea-county-for-data-center-campus/2902150, the company page https://www.newerainfra.ai/locations and the permit harvest. Nothing states Backup Power or the engine counts, so leave it as no until a permit appears.
  - links: https://www.businesswire.com/news/home/20251106765936/en/New-Era-Energy-Digital-Enters-into-Land-Option-Purchase-Agreement-for-3500-Acres-in-New-Mexico-for-7GW-AI-Data-Center-Hub-Marks-first-Wholly-Owned-Development-Separate-from-TCDC-Joint-Venture (ok=None, contains_value=None)
  - links: https://www.abqjournal.com/business/texas-company-eyes-lea-county-for-data-center-campus/2902150 (ok=None, contains_value=None)
  - links: https://www.newerainfra.ai/locations (ok=None, contains_value=None)
- notes: The permit harvest file _data_centers.md lists this plant as 'none found' for permits.

### G100001090367 / Lea County Data Center power station / Phase 1
*question about existing data; checklist rows 7 (Validation report errors fixed)*
- about: validation
- question: Is there a document that states the technology for the Lea County Data Center?

### G100001092797 / PNM 2023 IRP power station 3 / 1, timepoint 2 IRP
*question about existing data; checklist rows 7 (Validation report errors fixed)*
- about: validation
- question: Does any document state Backup Power for these placeholder turbines?
- finding: I read PDF pages 94 to 95 and 149 to 152 of the 2023 plan at https://www.pnm.com/documents/d/pnm.com/pnm-2023-irp-final-pdf. New turbines are modeled as firm capacity for the grid, with no backup role.
- recommendation: Leave Backup Power at no on both units. I found no document that states the value either way.
  - links: https://www.pnm.com/documents/d/pnm.com/pnm-2023-irp-final-pdf (ok=None, contains_value=None)
- notes: I searched the 2023 plan and the 2026 plan for backup power wording on these units and found none.

### G100001102080 / Roswell Information Park Data Center power station / 1
*question about existing data; checklist rows 7 (Validation report errors fixed)*
- about: validation
- question: Does any document state the turbine technology for the Roswell plant?
- finding: I searched the October 2026 news story, the city of Roswell agendas and a web search for the developer. None names a turbine or engine type.
- recommendation: Leave Turbine/Engine Technology as unknown and check again when an air permit application appears.
  - links: https://www.abqjournal.com/business/data-center-developers-are-eyeing-new-mexico-will-they-be-welcomed/3147510 (ok=None, contains_value=None)
- notes: No air permit for this plant was found in the state record harvest.

### G100001092804 / SPS 2023 IRP power plants / 1 IRP
*question about existing data; checklist rows 7 (Validation report errors fixed)*
- about: validation
- question: Does any document state Backup Power for this placeholder?
- finding: I read the Siemens release at https://www.siemens-energy.com/us/en/home/press-releases/Siemens-Energy-to-supply-ten-gas-turbines-to-Xcel-Energy.html, the Utility Dive story at https://www.utilitydive.com/news/new-mexico-regulators-approve-spss-9b-gas-heavy-resource-plan/820978 and the 2026 plan's resource tables. The Siemens release uses the words 'backup energy' for Tolk in a loose grid sense, and calls Gaines County a 'peaker plant'. No document describes an emergency backup role.
- recommendation: Leave Backup Power at no. Re-check when the Texas scope adds the two plants.
  - links: https://www.siemens-energy.com/us/en/home/press-releases/Siemens-Energy-to-supply-ten-gas-turbines-to-Xcel-Energy.html (ok=None, contains_value=None)
  - links: https://www.utilitydive.com/news/new-mexico-regulators-approve-spss-9b-gas-heavy-resource-plan/820978 (ok=None, contains_value=None)
- notes: I found no document that states the value either way.

### G100000414180 / Valencia energy facility / CC_set
*question about existing data; checklist rows 7 (Validation report errors fixed)*
- about: validation
- question: Is there any document that states the Backup Power value for the Valencia units?
- finding: I searched the September 2026 EIA-860M table, the PNM 2023 resource plan and the PNM 2024 press release. None states a backup power role. All describe the plant as grid peaking capacity.
- recommendation: Leave Backup Power as no for all five units. No document was found that states it, so the Data Source box stays empty.
  - links: https://www.eia.gov/electricity/data/eia860m/xls/september_generator2026.xlsx (ok=None, contains_value=None)
  - links: https://www.pnm.com/documents/d/pnm.com/pnm-2023-irp-final-pdf (ok=None, contains_value=None)
  - links: https://www.prnewswire.com/news-releases/pnm-files-application-for-new-resources-in-2028-302314788.html (ok=None, contains_value=None)
- notes: Opened https://www.eia.gov/electricity/data/eia860m/xls/september_generator2026.xlsx , https://www.pnm.com/documents/d/pnm.com/pnm-2023-irp-final-pdf and https://www.prnewswire.com/news-releases/pnm-files-application-for-new-resources-in-2028-302314788.html . Backup power is a data center concept and this plant serves PNM's grid.

### G100001094076 / Lordsburg Generating Station / GT1
*question about existing data*
- about: Capacity (MW)
- question: Should Lordsburg capacity be the 44.0 MW nameplate and not the 41.3 MW summer figure?

- recommendation: Leave 41.3 MW if the team accepts it, or change both units to 44.0 MW, the EIA nameplate. Ask the project manager which figure to keep, since the permit gives a third value.

### Milagro power station
*question about existing data; checklist rows 8 (No blanks for fuel, status, technology, country, coordinates, accuracy, owner)*
- about: Status
- question: Did the Milagro turbines retire in 2016, as the federal file says, or stop in 2022, as the Southwest Power Pool study says?
- finding: The federal generator file for September 2026 puts Milagro Cogeneration Plant 54814 on its Retired sheet with March 2016. The Southwest Power Pool study for the 60 megawatt Milagro request says Harvest Midstream's two 30 megawatt turbines stopped in 2022 and will return through request FEUS-1 in June 2027.
- recommendation: Keep the add as mothballed with the 2022 step, since the owner plans a return; change to retired only if the owner withdraws FEUS-1.
  - links: https://www.eia.gov/electricity/data/eia860m/xls/september_generator2026.xlsx (ok=True, contains_value=True)
  - links: https://opsportal.spp.org/documents/studies/files/2024_Generation_Studies/Milagro_60MW_IFS_Report_1.0.pdf (ok=True, contains_value=True)
- notes: Federal file, Retired sheet, plant 54814. Southwest Power Pool Milagro study, project description section on the first pages.

### G100001092790 / PNM 2023 IRP power station 1 / 1, timepoint 1 IRP
*change to an existing unit; checklist rows 10 (Every unit in development updated, with a Last Updated entry), 20 (No start year on cancelled or shelved projects), 37 (IRP box ticked on every IRP project (US only)), 50 (Other last-minute status changes checked)*
**Action:** Set Status of unit 1, timepoint 1 IRP (G100001092790) to cancelled. It now says announced. Add the link below to the Status Data Source box. Keep the links already there.
- `Status`: `announced` -> `cancelled`
- milestone `cancelled`: September 2026 (https://www.pnm.com/documents/d/pnm.com/pnm-2026-irp_final_report_only_sc-pdf)
  - Status Data Source: https://www.pnm.com/documents/d/pnm.com/pnm-2026-irp_final_report_only_sc-pdf (ok=True, contains_value=True)
- confidence: medium (independent: False)
- notes: This placeholder comes from the 2023 resource plan of Public Service Company of New Mexico. The newer 2026 plan no longer carries it. 2023 plan: - Open https://www.pnm.com/documents/d/pnm.com/pnm-2023-irp-final-pdf and go to PDF page 218, which is printed page 210. Table 69, Most-Cost Effective Portfolio, shows the Natural Gas row rising to 832 MW in 2037, from 700 MW the year before. That is the 132 MW in 2037. - The same table shows Natural Gas at zero in 2040 and a Hydrogen CT row of 586 MW in 2040. These are the timepoint 2 conversion units. 2026 plan: - Open https://www.pnm.com/documents/d/pnm.com/pnm-2026-irp_final_report_only_sc-pdf and go to PDF page 177, printed page 162, in the table of modeling assumptions. The New Resources Pending Approval row says 'A new 172 MW combustion turbine is included as a proxy unit in 2029 in all portfolios.' That is the only new gas unit in the plan before 2036. - PDF page 229, Table 9-1 continued, lists Combustion Turbines Gas/H2 as 0 MW through 2036 and 179 MW at most through 2045. No 2037, 2038 or 2039 gas unit of this size appears. - PDF page 177 also says 'Operating life of Reeves (146 MW) is extended through 2044' and that other existing gas units stay in service through 2044. So the 2040 retirement date no longer holds either. Why cancelled and not renamed: - The 2026 plan filed on September 1, 2026 replaces the 2023 plan. Its new gas placeholder is 172 MW in 2029, not this size or year. The queue row IA-PNM-2025-20 at the Reeves substation matches that 172 MW unit, not this one. - This is one document, so the status change is medium confidence. Timepoint 1 unit of the 2023 plan placeholder, the natural gas phase. The 2026 plan drops the placeholder, so the status is reported as cancelled. The 2023 plan only says its cost assumptions rest on a GE LM6000 combustion turbine, on PDF page 151, and gives no site or model for this unit.

### G100000403560 / Maddox power station / 2
*question about existing data; checklist rows 21 (Planned retirements searched), 43 (Ownership changes for operating units reviewed (optional)), 50 (Other last-minute status changes checked)*

- notes: Open case 25-00076-UT at https://e360.prc.nm.gov/portal/public/#/public/nm-prc/en/CaseXscreen?screen=external-Case360&caseId=fe3141c2-1770-4ee1-90a7-f9c3a4749200, filing DOC-000532299-26, PDF page 47, Table 3-1, under Grid Balancing Resources, Gas, the row "Maddox Unit 2, Lea Co., NM, 62, 2043". The 2023 plan at https://www.xcelenergy.com/staticfiles/xe-responsive/Company/Rates%20&%20Regulations/Resource%20Plans/2023%20SPS-IRP%20Plan.pdf says "3-year extension of Maddox Unit 2 from 2025 to 2028". The EIA table at https://www.eia.gov/electricity/data/eia860m/xls/september_generator2026.xlsx still shows December 2034. The portal could not be read by the verifier, so no value is proposed. Its link to the plan document, https://xcelnew.my.salesforce.com/sfc/p/1U0000011ttV/a/R300000YZWcY/_fFYXD6bn_rleQjhaRJ9XZ_GHjexUzC57fARqr3lTDc, did not load any text that names Maddox, so it could not be cited. A web search and the PRC news release found no readable copy of the table either. The saved case copy shows the same table. The Texas filing that confirms the Maddox Unit 1 extension, docket 58664 (Eric Tucker's direct testimony at https://interchange.puc.texas.gov/Documents/58664_13_1542660.PDF), covers only unit 1, so it does not settle unit 2.

### G100001048439 / NTEC Gas power plant / GEN13-18
*question about existing data; checklist rows 10 (Every unit in development updated, with a Last Updated entry)*
- about: Capacity (MW)
- question: Should NTEC capacity change from 60.5 MW to 41.22 MW per turbine?
- finding: The EPA notice gives 742 MW from eighteen turbines, each driving a 41.22 MW generator. GEM and the EIA table show 60.5 MW for each of 18 generators, which is 1,089 MW.
- recommendation: Change each single unit GEN1 to GEN12 to 41.2 MW and GEN13-18 to 247.3 MW with Capacity Per Engine 41.2, because the permit gives the generator rating and the manual records electrical nameplate. Keep the unit naming as it is, since all 18 turbines are covered.
  - links: https://web.archive.org/web/20260421215236/https://www.epa.gov/publicnotices/ntec-energy-generation-plant (ok=None, contains_value=None)
  - links: https://www.eia.gov/electricity/data/eia860m/xls/september_generator2026.xlsx (ok=None, contains_value=None)
  - links: https://downloads.regulations.gov/EPA-R09-OAR-2024-0028-0155/content.pdf (ok=None, contains_value=None)
- notes: Open the saved EPA notice at https://web.archive.org/web/20260421215236/https://www.epa.gov/publicnotices/ntec-energy-generation-plant. In the Proposed Action section, the paragraph that starts 'This draft permit, if finalized, will authorize' says 'new 742-megawatt (MW) natural gas-fired power plant' and 'Each combustion turbine will drive a 41.22-MW electrical generator'. Eighteen times 41.22 is 741.96 MW. The EIA-860M table for September 2026 at https://www.eia.gov/electricity/data/eia860m/xls/september_generator2026.xlsx lists 60.5 MW nameplate, 37.6 MW summer and 41.4 MW winter for each of GEN1 to GEN18 on the Planned sheet. The 60.5 MW is probably the turbine rating before the generator. The final permit of August 11, 2026 at https://downloads.regulations.gov/EPA-R09-OAR-2024-0028-0155/content.pdf, Table 1 on page 6, gives the same figures: 'Rated Power Output: 41.22 MW Net-Basis' for each General Electric LM-6000 turbine GEN001 to GEN018 and a 'Combined maximum design capacity of 742 MW Net-Basis'.

### G100001048439 / NTEC Gas power plant / GEN13-18
*question about existing data; checklist rows 10 (Every unit in development updated, with a Last Updated entry), 50 (Other last-minute status changes checked)*
- about: Status
- question: Is the final NTEC permit issued on August 11, 2026 the same project as the draft?
- finding: The EPA actions table at the saved copy lists the NTEC Energy Generation Plant docket EPA-R09-OAR-2024-0028 as Issued on 2026/08/11. No construction start was found.
- recommendation: Leave the status as pre-construction and add the permit date to Status Detail. Move to construction only when a source reports work under way.
  - links: https://web.archive.org/web/20261104114042/https://www.epa.gov/caa-permitting/r9-caa-permit-actions (ok=None, contains_value=None)
  - links: https://downloads.regulations.gov/EPA-R09-OAR-2024-0028-0155/content.pdf (ok=None, contains_value=None)
- notes: Open https://web.archive.org/web/20261104114042/https://www.epa.gov/caa-permitting/r9-caa-permit-actions and find the row 'NTEC Energy Generation Plant'. The final permit at https://downloads.regulations.gov/EPA-R09-OAR-2024-0028-0155/content.pdf, Table 1 on page 6, keeps eighteen turbines at 41.22 MW each, 742 MW in all. Neither the permit nor any news story found reports construction under way.

### G100001092790 / PNM 2023 IRP power station 1 / 1, timepoint 1 IRP
*question about existing data; checklist rows 10 (Every unit in development updated, with a Last Updated entry), 20 (No start year on cancelled or shelved projects), 37 (IRP box ticked on every IRP project (US only)), 50 (Other last-minute status changes checked)*
- about: Status
- question: Should this 2023 plan placeholder be cancelled now that the 2026 plan drops it?
- finding: The 2023 plan at https://www.pnm.com/documents/d/pnm.com/pnm-2023-irp-final-pdf adds 132 MW in 2037 on PDF page 218, Table 69. The 2026 plan at https://www.pnm.com/documents/d/pnm.com/pnm-2026-irp_final_report_only_sc-pdf has no gas unit of that size or year and adds only a 172 MW proxy unit in 2029 on PDF page 177.
- recommendation: Accept the cancelled status and the cancellation year of 2026 for both units of this plant. If you accept it, also remove the start year and the planned retirement year by hand, since a cancelled unit carries neither.
  - links: https://www.pnm.com/documents/d/pnm.com/pnm-2026-irp_final_report_only_sc-pdf (ok=None, contains_value=None)
  - links: https://www.pnm.com/documents/d/pnm.com/pnm-2023-irp-final-pdf (ok=None, contains_value=None)
  - links: https://emp.lbl.gov/sites/default/files/2026-05/LBNL_Ix_Queue_Data_File_thru2025.xlsx (ok=None, contains_value=None)
- notes: Open https://www.pnm.com/documents/d/pnm.com/pnm-2026-irp_final_report_only_sc-pdf, PDF page 177, row New Resources Pending Approval, and PDF page 229, the table of resource additions. The queue row IA-PNM-2025-20 for 172 MW of gas at the Reeves substation in 2029 is in the Lawrence Berkeley National Laboratory queue file at https://emp.lbl.gov/sites/default/files/2026-05/LBNL_Ix_Queue_Data_File_thru2025.xlsx. It matches the 172 MW proxy unit, so it should be handled as one new unit with the Reeves plant, not by renaming this record.

### G100001090997 / Project Jupiter Microgrid power station / East
*question about existing data*
- about: Start year
- question: Should the 2026 start year on cancelled unit East be cleared?
- finding: The unit is cancelled and still carries a start year of 2026. The manual says cancelled units never carry a start year. The cancellation holds, as the Oracle press release at https://www.oracle.com/news/announcement/oracle-borderplex-and-bloom-energy-to-power-project-jupiter-with-fuel-cell-technology-2026-04-27/ and the Source NM article at https://sourcenm.com/2026/04/27/nm-project-jupiter-data-center-developers-announce-new-plans-for-generating-power/ both say the gas turbines were replaced on April 27, 2026.
- recommendation: Clear the Start year on the East unit in the web form and leave Cancellation year as 2026.

### G100000402824 / Rio Grande (New Mexico) power station / 7
*question about existing data; checklist rows 21 (Planned retirements searched), 37 (IRP box ticked on every IRP project (US only)), 43 (Ownership changes for operating units reviewed (optional)), 50 (Other last-minute status changes checked)*
- about: Planned retire
- question: Is Rio Grande unit 7 still due to retire in 2027?
- finding: The commission's final order of April 4, 2025 allows El Paso Electric to abandon unit 7 and hold it in contingency reserve until it is retired. The EIA table for September 2026 still lists unit 7 as operating with a planned retirement in December 2027.
- recommendation: Leave the status as operating and the planned retirement as 2027. Ask the project manager whether the unit should be marked mothballed once El Paso Electric reports it in contingency reserve.
  - links: https://epelectric.com/el-paso-electric/uploads/notice-for-23-00403-ut.pdf (ok=None, contains_value=None)
  - links: https://www.epelectric.com/el-paso-electric/uploads/2026_epe_system_expansion_plan_2027-2036.pdf (ok=None, contains_value=None)
  - links: https://www.eia.gov/electricity/data/eia860m/xls/september_generator2026.xlsx (ok=None, contains_value=None)
  - links: https://www.epelectric.com/files/24-00260-UT-2025-09-02-EPE-Integrated-Resource-Plan-Report.pdf (ok=None, contains_value=None)
- notes: Opened the case notice at https://epelectric.com/el-paso-electric/uploads/notice-for-23-00403-ut.pdf (page 1, paragraph 1, asks to abandon Rio Grande unit 7 and Newman unit 1 by January 1, 2026) and a New Mexico Political Report article at https://nmpoliticalreport.com/2025/03/06/el-paso-electric-wants-to-replace-aging-natural-gas-generation-with-renewables/ (says the commission did not vote and the chairman said it will likely be approved). I could not find the commission's final order; the commission's case guest index did not show the case in the earlier harvest.

Final order:
- Open El Paso Electric's 2025 resource plan at https://www.epelectric.com/files/24-00260-UT-2025-09-02-EPE-Integrated-Resource-Plan-Report.pdf and go to the footnote 11 under Table 34 at the page printed as 114. It says 'Per the Commission's Final Order, EPE may abandon RG7 and Newman 1 according to the plan submitted in its Application' and cites 'Final Order Granting Abandonment, Ordering Paragraph B (Apr. 4, 2025)'.
- The same plan's Table 34 lists Rio Grande 7 with accredited capacity of 0 MW.
- I could not open the order itself because the commission's case pages are drawn by scripts and the order has no address of its own.

### G100001102080 / Roswell Information Park Data Center power station / 1
*question about existing data; checklist rows 10 (Every unit in development updated, with a Last Updated entry), 39 (Gas-powered data centers searched, captive data entered (US only))*
- about: Capacity (MW)
- question: Should capacity stay 700 MW given the 736 MW plant offer?
- finding: The story at https://www.abqjournal.com/business/data-center-developers-are-eyeing-new-mexico-will-they-be-welcomed/3147510 says the company will build 700 megawatts and has an offer on a 736-megawatt gas plant.
- recommendation: Keep 700 MW. Change it only if a permit or a signed purchase names a different electrical size.
- notes: One tracker lists 200 MW for this campus, but I did not use it because it gives no document. The city agenda at https://www.roswell-nm.gov/AgendaCenter/ViewFile/Agenda/_02032026-2981 names the campus but gives no megawatts. The agenda is a scanned PDF that the verifier could not read, so I did not cite it.

### G100001092804 / SPS 2023 IRP power plants / 1 IRP
*question about existing data; checklist rows 10 (Every unit in development updated, with a Last Updated entry), 37 (IRP box ticked on every IRP project (US only)), 38 (GEM IDs matched to EIA-860M, EIP and Sierra Club (US only))*
- about: Capacity (MW)
- question: What is the 233 MW planned gas unit on the Sierra Club list?
- finding: Table 3-3 on PDF page 49 of the 2026 plan at https://e360.prc.nm.gov/portal/public/#/public/nm-prc/en/CaseXscreen?screen=external-Case360&caseId=fe3141c2-1770-4ee1-90a7-f9c3a4749200 lists 'Dispatchable Gas Thermal - Tolk Unit 5' at 232.5 MW in Bailey County, Texas, for December 2029. That matches the 233 MW.
- recommendation: Give it to the Texas scope as a separate planned unit at Tolk Station. Do not add it to this New Mexico unit.

### G100001101987 / Site Layer 1 Data Center power station / 1
*question about existing data; checklist rows 10 (Every unit in development updated, with a Last Updated entry), 39 (Gas-powered data centers searched, captive data entered (US only))*
- about: Capacity (MW)
- question: Does the 601.6 MW and 64 generators figure for Site Layer I apply to this plant?
- finding: The application at https://service.web.env.nm.gov/urls/GyvVdjHy lists only 32 engines, ENG01 to ENG32, on PDF page 12, and PDF page 36 says 32. The harvest's 64 and 601.6 MW is double the real count.
- recommendation: Keep 32 engines and 300.8 MW. Do not add a second plant.
- notes: The equipment list on PDF page 12 also has one extra row, SSM1, for start-up emissions. It is not a separate generator. The incompleteness letter at https://service.web.env.nm.gov/urls/WxDewWXD names no engines.

### G100000403710 / Valencia energy facility / CTG1
*question about existing data; checklist rows 21 (Planned retirements searched), 43 (Ownership changes for operating units reviewed (optional)), 50 (Other last-minute status changes checked)*
- about: Planned retire
- question: Should the 2028 planned retirement for CTG1 stay, given the approved extension of the Valencia power purchase agreement?
- finding: PNM's 2023 resource plan says the Valencia power purchase agreement expires in 2028. A PNM press release from 2024 says PNM asked the state commission to extend it for 167 MW through 2039. A TXNM Energy news item says the commission approved an unopposed stipulation on this application on June 26, 2025.
- recommendation: Remove the planned retirement year of 2028 or change it to 2039, and add a note that the plant is expected to keep operating under the extended agreement. Check the commission order in case 24-00271-UT first.
  - links: https://www.pnm.com/documents/d/pnm.com/pnm-2023-irp-final-pdf (ok=None, contains_value=None)
  - links: https://www.prnewswire.com/news-releases/pnm-files-application-for-new-resources-in-2028-302314788.html (ok=None, contains_value=None)
  - links: https://www.gurufocus.com/news/2951980/approval-of-unopposed-stipulation-in-pnm-2028-resource-application (ok=None, contains_value=None)
- notes: Sources:
- PNM 2023 resource plan, https://www.pnm.com/documents/d/pnm.com/pnm-2023-irp-final-pdf , section 4.1.3, Valencia Energy Facility: 'The PPA expires in 2028.'
- PNM press release, https://www.prnewswire.com/news-releases/pnm-files-application-for-new-resources-in-2028-302314788.html : 'extension of a Valencia Purchase Power Agreement (PPA) for 167 MW through 2039 (current agreement expires in 2028)'.
- News item on the approval, https://www.gurufocus.com/news/2951980/approval-of-unopposed-stipulation-in-pnm-2028-resource-application : approval of the unopposed stipulation, which includes the Valencia agreement through 2039.
- A power purchase agreement ending is not a plant retirement. EIA lists no planned retirement. I did not find the commission's final order text.

### G100001102061 / Westside power station / 1
*question about existing data; checklist rows 10 (Every unit in development updated, with a Last Updated entry), 49 (Construction to operating checked)*
- about: Capacity (MW)
- question: Should Westside capacity stay at 63 MW when EIA lists 60 MW?
- finding: The EIA-860M table for September 2026 lists three generators of 16, 24 and 20 MW, which is 60 MW. GEM has 63 MW, which is six 3 MW engines plus ten 4.5 MW engines. The permit gives engine power only in horsepower.
- recommendation: Leave 63 MW as is unless a document gives the electrical rating of the generator sets. Ask the PM whether to use the EIA 60 MW.
  - links: https://www.eia.gov/electricity/data/eia860m/xls/september_generator2026.xlsx (ok=None, contains_value=None)
  - links: https://cloud.env.nm.gov/resources/_translator.php/NWFjY2UwMjdjMmQwODZiNzdjZjc0OTYzNF8yMzgzOTA~.pdf (ok=None, contains_value=None)
- notes: EIA file: https://www.eia.gov/electricity/data/eia860m/xls/september_generator2026.xlsx, sheet Planned, plant 70261, rows WS01 16.0, WS02 24.0, WS03 20.0 MW, planned operation March 2027, January 2028 and May 2028. The permit https://cloud.env.nm.gov/resources/_translator.php/NWFjY2UwMjdjMmQwODZiNzdjZjc0OTYzNF8yMzgzOTA~.pdf shows the J620 at 3,713 horsepower and the J624 at 5,771 horsepower in the original table, and 4,581 and 6,161 horsepower in the Statement of Basis at https://cloud.env.nm.gov/resources/_translator.php/ZjIxMzI5OGU3OWMxZTliZGY4YmJlZThmNV8yMzgzODU~.pdf. Neither states electrical MW as a clear figure. I did not find a Jenbacher data sheet in the budget.

## Checklist group 4. Conversions

Coal-to-gas and other conversions: unit naming, fuel and technology, replacement links.

### G100001092790 / PNM 2023 IRP power station 1 / 1, timepoint 1 IRP
*question about existing data; checklist rows 12 (Conversions carry "timepoint" in the unit name)*
- about: Unit name
- question: What should the unit name 1, timepoint 1 IRP be changed to?
- finding: The name 1, timepoint 1 IRP does not follow the timepoint form of a name, a comma, a space and the word timepoint with a number. The extra word IRP is the only part that breaks the form.
- recommendation: Rename it to 1, timepoint 1 so it follows the form. If the unit is cancelled, the name can still be fixed the same way.
- notes: This is a naming rule from the batch brief. No outside document is needed.

### G100001092790 / PNM 2023 IRP power station 1 / 1, timepoint 1 IRP
*question about existing data; checklist rows 12 (Conversions carry "timepoint" in the unit name), 13 (Coal-to-gas conversions have gas fuel and technology)*
- about: Conversion/replacement?
- question: Should the timepoint 1 unit keep a blank conversion field?
- finding: The 2023 plan at https://www.pnm.com/documents/d/pnm.com/pnm-2023-irp-final-pdf, PDF page 151, says new turbines run on natural gas and are assumed to convert to hydrogen in 2040. Timepoint 1 is the gas phase, so it replaces and converts nothing.
- recommendation: Leave the conversion fields blank on the timepoint 1 unit, because no earlier unit is named. The conversion is recorded on the timepoint 2 unit, which already points back to G100001092790.
  - links: https://www.pnm.com/documents/d/pnm.com/pnm-2023-irp-final-pdf (ok=None, contains_value=None)
- notes: Open the plan and read the paragraph above Table 45 on PDF page 151, printed page 143.

*change to an existing unit; checklist rows 43 (Ownership changes for operating units reviewed (optional)), 50 (Other last-minute status changes checked)*

Together they equal the 287 MW GEM shows. The September 2026 EIA table shows the unit operating. PNM's 2026 resource plan gives a planned retirement year of 2042, with 2044 used only for modeling. ### G100000401761 / Chino Mines power station / CTG9
*change to an existing unit; checklist rows 43 (Ownership changes for operating units reviewed (optional)), 50 (Other last-minute status changes checked)*
**Action:** Leave Status of unit CTG9 (G100000401761) as operating. It is still correct. Add the link below to the Status Data Source box. Keep the links already there.
- `Status`: `operating` -> `operating`
  - Status Data Source: https://www.eia.gov/electricity/data/eia860m/xls/september_generator2026.xlsx (ok=True, contains_value=True)
- confidence: medium (independent: False)
- notes: Open the EIA-860M generator table for September 2026 at https://www.eia.gov/electricity/data/eia860m/xls/september_generator2026.xlsx and go to the Operating sheet. Plant 54667 Chino Mines has generator 9 (unit code CTG9) at 37.5 MW with combined cycle technology, operating year 2001, and generator 7 at 16.5 MW, operating year 1959. Both are marked status SB, standby or backup, available for service but not normally used. EIA still shows the plant in the Operating sheet, so it has not been retired or listed with a retirement date. The standby flag was already weighed in the Status Detail text. I found no retirement, mothballing or sale news in a web search. Note that the EIA label is standby, so the reviewer may prefer to keep the existing decision. The plant is still in the EIA operating list as standby and I found no retirement or owner change. I could not find a document that names the turbine model. EIA does not list it and a web search found only plant database pages. I also found no document that states backup power.

### G100001094076 / Lordsburg Generating Station / GT1
*question about existing data; checklist rows 19 (Missing or unknown owner searched), 43 (Ownership changes for operating units reviewed (optional)), 50 (Other last-minute status changes checked)*
- about: Owner(s)
- question: Should the Lordsburg owner be Public Service Company of New Mexico, not PNM Resources Inc?
- finding: The Acid Rain permit application names Public Service Company of New Mexico as "Facility Owner and Operator". The EIA-860M table for September 2026 lists the owner as Public Service Co of NM. The database shows PNM Resources Inc, which is the holding company.
- recommendation: Change Owner(s) on both units to Public Service Company of New Mexico [100%], reusing the entity that already exists. Add EIA 7967 to Other IDs (location).

EIA table: https://www.eia.gov/electricity/data/eia860m/xls/september_generator2026.xlsx, Operating sheet, plant 7967, entity name "Public Service Co of NM". Capacity is the EIA nameplate of 44 MW, not PNM's lower operating figure.

### G100000403421 / Reeves power station / 3
*question about existing data; checklist rows 37 (IRP box ticked on every IRP project (US only)), 51 (Newly announced projects checked)*
- about: existence
- question: Is the 172 MW gas request at the Reeves substation a new unit at Reeves?
- finding: PNM queue request IA-PNM-2025-20 is a 172 MW gas generator with in-service date January 2029 and point of interconnection "Reeves Substation". The 2026 PNM plan carries a 172 MW combustion turbine in 2029 only as a proxy unit with no site named.
- recommendation: Accept the new unit 4 record in this batch as announced, since one verified queue entry is enough to add. Move it to its own plant if a later PNM filing places the turbine at another site.
  - links: https://www.pnm.com/documents/d/pnm.com/pnm-2026-irp_final_report_only_sc-pdf (ok=True, contains_value=True)
  - links: https://emp.lbl.gov/sites/default/files/2026-05/LBNL_Ix_Queue_Data_File_thru2025.xlsx (ok=True, contains_value=True)
- notes: Queue row: Berkeley Lab queue file https://emp.lbl.gov/sites/default/files/2026-05/LBNL_Ix_Queue_Data_File_thru2025.xlsx, request IA-PNM-2025-20, which I read from the saved harvest and could not run through the verifier. Plan: https://www.pnm.com/documents/d/pnm.com/pnm-2026-irp_final_report_only_sc-pdf, the table of modeling assumptions, New Resources Pending Approval: "A new 172 MW combustion turbine is included as a proxy unit in 2029 in all portfolios. Actual resource selection will be determined though a 2029-2032 All-Source RFP Supplement". Scenario 12 in the same report removes this unit. A second check of the plan found: The Berkeley Lab queue file lists IA-PNM-2025-20, 172 megawatts of gas at the Reeves Substation for January 2029, and PNM's 2026 plan carries a 172 megawatt proxy turbine in 2029 in every portfolio except Scenario 12. The Reeves plant agent advised holding it because it could not open the queue file. Accept the unit 4 add as announced; if the 2029 to 2032 request for proposals picks another site, move the unit to that site.

In July 2020 the commission approved an all solar and storage replacement, reported by Utility Dive, and the gas plant was dropped. The federal generator file for September 2026 still lists plant 63281, Pinon Energy Center, with seven 41.8 megawatt turbines on its Canceled or Postponed sheet, with the point used here. The PNM queue requests IA-PNM-2017-14 and IA-PNM-2020-05 at San Juan, in the Berkeley Lab queue file, fit this plant. The cancellation was in 2020, inside the period GEM tracks, so the plant is added as cancelled. Only one publisher reports the cancellation, so the status needs a second source to be fully confirmed.

What the sources say:
- EIA-860M September 2026 at https://www.eia.gov/electricity/data/eia860m/xls/september_generator2026.xlsx, Operating sheet, plant 55977: generators RICE3 and RICE4, each 9.4 MW nameplate, technology Natural Gas Internal Combustion Engine, status OP, operating month 12 and year 2025.
- A Navajo Times article from February 2026 at https://navajotimes.com/reznews/farmington-completes-bluffview-power-upgrade-after-san-juan-closure: the caption and fourth paragraph say two Wärtsilä 20V34SG engines, about 9 megawatts each, about 18 megawatts total, with a ribbon cutting on January 14, 2026 that marked the start of commercial operation.
- A Power Engineering article at https://www.power-eng.com/onsite-power/wartsila-to-supply-rice-engines-for-new-mexico-plant-expansion/ reports the Wärtsilä contract for an 18 MW expansion at the plant.

Start year: the EIA table says December 2025 and the city says commercial operation began in January 2026. Start year 2025 follows the EIA table, so check which the reviewer prefers.

Not found: a separate source stating the owner for the engines. The EIA table lists the City of Farmington as the plant owner.

The plant total is 85.8 MW with the combined cycle unit, so the plant stays above the threshold.

### Belen IA-PNM-2026-15 gas power station
*question about existing data*
- about: duplicate
- question: Are the two new gas requests at PNM's Belen substation, IA-PNM-2026-15 and IA-PNM-2026-19, new plants, or additions to the La Luz or Valencia plants near Belen?
- finding: PNM's October 2026 request list shows 172 megawatts of gas for December 2029 and a 100.50 megawatt solar, battery and gas request for December 2030, both at the Belen 115 kV Substation, with no applicant named. The La Luz plant and the Valencia plant both sit near Belen, but PNM's 2026 plan gives only 40 megawatts of new gas at La Luz, for 2031.
- recommendation: Accept both adds as announced with the owner blank; if a later filing ties either request to La Luz or Valencia, move it there as a new unit.
  - links: https://www.oasis.oati.com/woa/docs/PNM/PNMdocs/10_October_2026_Interconnection_Matrix_Posted_10-30-26.pdf (ok=True, contains_value=True)
- notes: PNM request list, page 2, rows IA-PNM-2026-15 and IA-PNM-2026-19: https://www.oasis.oati.com/woa/docs/PNM/PNMdocs/10_October_2026_Interconnection_Matrix_Posted_10-30-26.pdf. La Luz unit 2 is in Table 1-6 on PDF page 45 of the PNM 2026 plan. Valencia's own requests in the same list are IA-PNM-2001-12, IA-PNM-2007-12 and IA-PNM-2012-01, all in service. A web search found only queue copies on interconnection.fyi.

### G100000403559 / Maddox power station / 1

- about: scope
- question: Should Maddox Unit 3, an 11.5 MW gas turbine, be added?

### G100001092804 / SPS 2023 IRP power plants / 1 IRP
*question about existing data*
- about: scope
- question: Should this 2,088 MW placeholder become two Texas plants?
- finding: The Siemens Energy release at https://www.siemens-energy.com/us/en/home/press-releases/Siemens-Energy-to-supply-ten-gas-turbines-to-Xcel-Energy.html and Table 3-2 on PDF pages 47 and 48 of the 2026 plan at https://e360.prc.nm.gov/portal/public/#/public/nm-prc/en/CaseXscreen?screen=external-Case360&caseId=fe3141c2-1770-4ee1-90a7-f9c3a4749200 give Gaines County, Texas at 1,160 MW and Tolk Station, Texas at 928 MW. Together they equal 2,088 MW.
- recommendation: Send both plants to the Texas scope as two adds, then cancel or remove this New Mexico placeholder. Keep this unit as it is until Texas has them.
- notes: The Utility Dive story at https://www.utilitydive.com/news/new-mexico-regulators-approve-spss-9b-gas-heavy-resource-plan/820978 confirms the New Mexico commission approved 2,088 MW of gas on May 7, 2026. The New Mexico case 25-00066-UT is not in the commission's public case index, so I could not read the order itself. Neither plant is in New Mexico, so no New Mexico point or city applies.

### G100001094233 / SWPP ERAS Hobbs power station / 1
*question about existing data*
- about: duplicate
- question: Is this Hobbs turbine really the fifth turbine of the Gaines County Power Plant in Texas, which the database already has?
- finding: The grid operator's file for queue request ERAS-2025-014 holds an email from Southwestern Public Service saying the request is "utilizing the extra capacity available from the SPS Gaines County Power Plant thermal units". The Gaines plant has five gas turbines of 1,160 MW in total, four of them due by December 2028, and the 234 MW request is due June 2029 on the same Hobbs to Andrews 345 kV line. The database already lists Gaines County Power Plant at 1,160 MW.
- recommendation: Delete the SWPP ERAS Hobbs power station record and leave the Gaines County Power Plant as it is, since its 1,160 MW already counts this turbine.
  - links: https://opsportal.spp.org/documents/studies/files/2025_Generation_Studies/ERAS-2025-014%20FS%20Draft%20-%20Combined.pdf (ok=None, contains_value=None)
  - links: https://opsportal.spp.org/Studies/GenerateActiveCSV (ok=None, contains_value=None)
  - links: https://interchange.puc.texas.gov/Documents/58664_2_1542635.PDF (ok=None, contains_value=None)
- notes: Where to find each piece:
- The Southwest Power Pool study file for ERAS-2025-014 at https://opsportal.spp.org/documents/studies/files/2025_Generation_Studies/ERAS-2025-014%20FS%20Draft%20-%20Combined.pdf. Page 1 is an email from SPS Transmission Planning. It says SPS will not submit a facility study for ERAS-2025-014 because it "is utilizing the extra capacity available from the SPS Gaines County Power Plant thermal units (GEN-2020-065)" and needs no new upgrades at the Sidewinder 345 kV substation.
- The open queue file at https://opsportal.spp.org/Studies/GenerateActiveCSV. Row ERAS-2025-014: nearest town Hobbs, combustion turbine, nameplate 234 MW, 159.5 MW of service, commercial operation 6/1/2029, Hobbs-Andrews 345kV Line. Row GEN-2020-065: nearest town Gaines, combined cycle, 1,003 MW, commercial operation 7/2/2028, the same Hobbs-Andrews 345 kV line.
- The Southwestern Public Service application to the Texas Public Utility Commission in docket 58664, filed in 2025, at https://interchange.puc.texas.gov/Documents/58664_2_1542635.PDF. The table on page 3 lists Gaines County at 1,160 MW and 1,003 MW of accredited capacity. Page 15 says the project should meet "an estimated December 1, 2028, in-service date for four of its five units". Five turbines at 232 MW each make the 1,160 MW the database already shows for Gaines.
- The match of the 234 MW request to the fifth Gaines turbine is my reading of these three documents. No document says it in one sentence.
- The Gaines plant is in Gaines County, Texas, south of Hobbs. The queue calls the place Hobbs because that is the nearest town.
- This record was created in the database on 2026-11-10 and is marked in progress, so check with the researcher working New Mexico before deleting it.
- Earlier I looked for New Mexico commission case 25-00066-UT. It is not in the commission's public index, and its public summary in a Utility Dive article from May 2026 at https://www.utilitydive.com/news/new-mexico-regulators-approve-sps-9b-gas-heavy-resource-plan/820978/ does not name Hobbs.
- I dropped the capacity, start year, technology and owner edits I had proposed for this record, since they would only fill out a likely duplicate.

### Todd CPG Pads 1 and 2
*watch item, new to the tracker; checklist rows 51 (Newly announced projects checked)*
- what to watch: Two Devon oil field turbine pads 600 meters apart, each about 38 megawatts; together about 75.
- check again by: 2027-03
  - links: https://cloud.env.nm.gov/resources/_translator.php/OThiMzI3YTA2OGM4MTEyOTQ1MjZkMjdlMF8xOTcxMTM~.pdf (ok=True, contains_value=False)
  - links: https://cloud.env.nm.gov/resources/_translator.php/ZWE5ZTk0Mzg3OWI1NDVmMzBiOGRlMGZiNF8xOTcxMTY~.pdf (ok=True, contains_value=False)
- notes: Devon Power New Mexico, LLC holds two state air permits for oil field power pads 600 meters apart in Lea County, 23.9 miles east-northeast of Malaga: Todd CPG Pad 1 (site 13016, permit 10513, approved March 26, 2025) and Todd CPG Pad 2 (site 13062, permit 10514, approved March 27, 2025). The equipment table in each application lists two Solar SMT130 trailer-mounted gas turbines of 21,024 horsepower and one Solar SMT60 of 7,402 horsepower. Both applications say the turbines make power only for Devon's own oil and gas sites. Solar's mobile power page rates the SMT130 at about 16 megawatts, so each pad is about 38 megawatts, under the 50 megawatt threshold, and the two together are about 75. These are rented trailer units under the general permit for oil and gas sites, and the permits treat the pads as two sites, so this is held as two pads under the threshold rather than one plant. If GEM counts neighboring pads as one plant, this becomes an add of about 75 megawatts. No document says the turbines are running. Devon's other pads, Cotton Draw (about 38 megawatts) and Rattlesnake East (about 28), are 23 kilometers or more away. Recheck in March 2027 for added turbines. Lea County posts no commission or planning agendas online. The City of Hobbs commission agendas and packets since 2024 (134 documents) and the Eddy County Commission agendas and minutes since 2024 were read; none names Devon Power or the Todd pads.
